# Review dimensions — consolidated report

> Read-only audit. 10 additional review lenses beyond the per-function bug/simplification sweep. No code changed.

## Summary

- **371 findings** across 10 lenses.
- Tail verification: **229 / 243** low candidate bugs confirmed real (14 refuted); simplifications **55 sound** / 27 marginal / 15 unsound.

## Numerical / domain correctness (33)

### [high] Float32 factor columns canonicalise non-dyadic decimals at f32 precision, so the SAME nominal value yields a different lookup key than a Float64 column or the authored string entry -> silent miss / wrong premium
- **Location:** `src/haute/_rating.py:346-370 (_rating_key_expr) vs 309-343 (normalise_rating_key); reached via _apply_rating_table:583,596`
- _rating_key_expr formats a float factor column to its key with `col.cast(pl.Utf8)`, which uses the COLUMN's native dtype precision (shortest round-trippable repr per dtype). A Float32 column therefore produces a different canonical key than a Float64 column for the same nominal value whenever that value is not exactly representable in f32 (i.e. almost every non-dyadic decimal). Because a rating table's entry keys are authored ONCE (as JSON strings after a sidecar round-trip, or as f64 JSON numbe...
- **Fix:** Make the float key canonicalisation width-independent so a value's key does not depend on whether its column is Float32 or Float64. In _rating_key_expr, for the non-int-like float branch, cast Float32 columns up to Float64 BEFORE the Utf8 cast (`col.cast(pl.Float64).cast(pl.Utf8)`) for both Float32 and Float64 dtypes, so the engine key matches the ...

### [high] Integer arithmetic computed in unbounded Python int, not Int64 — evaluator shows a wildly different number than Polars on overflow/wraparound
- **Location:** `src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1616-1635 (_ExprEvaluator._binop op_map: Add/Sub/Mult/Pow/Mod/FloorDiv via Python operator)`
- _binop dispatches integer +, -, *, ** through Python's operator module, which uses arbitrary-precision ints. Polars stores integer columns as Int64 (or narrower) and WRAPS on overflow (two's-complement). For any expression whose intermediate or final integer result exceeds 2**63-1 (or underflows), the trace evaluator prints the mathematically-exact bignum while the real pipeline produced a wrapped Int64. In a pricing context this is the textbook 'wrong number that does not crash': the explainabi...
- **Fix:** The evaluator is fundamentally dtype-unaware, so faithful Int64 wraparound cannot be reproduced from row values alone. Two defensible options: (a) when an integer arithmetic result falls outside the signed-64-bit range, do NOT display the bignum — return an explicit 'uncomputable / overflow' sentinel (None plus an error marker on the calculation) s...

### [high] Boolean & / | do not implement Kleene three-valued logic: `False & null` and `True | null` return None instead of False/True, mis-selecting when/then branches
- **Location:** `src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1616-1635 (_binop None-guard at 1619-1620) handling ast.BitAnd/ast.BitOr for boolean expressions`
- Polars uses Kleene (SQL three-valued) logic for boolean & and |: `False & null == False` and `True | null == True` (the determined operand dominates a null). The evaluator routes & and | through _binop, whose first action is the blanket null guard `if left is None or right is None: return None`. So any boolean combinator with one null operand collapses to None regardless of the other operand's truth value. This is directly load-bearing for pricing: the dominant conditional pattern is `pl.when(co...
- **Fix:** Before the generic None guard in _binop (or in a dedicated boolean path), special-case BitAnd/BitOr on boolean operands with Kleene semantics: for And, if either operand is False return False (even if the other is None); else if either is None return None; else left and right. For Or, if either operand is True return True; else if either is None re...

For a non-dyadic Float32 column value these two differ (engine uses 6-7 sig-digit f32 repr; mirror uses the 17-digit f64 repr of the f32-widened value). The mirror feeds trace enrichment (_trace_enrichment._enrich_single_table) and sidecar canonicalisation, so a trace can r...
- **Fix:** Fold this into the fix above by widening Float32->Float64 in _rating_key_expr (which makes engine == mirror for Float32 too), OR if Float32 keys must stay f32-formatted, give normalise_rating_key an explicit dtype-aware entry point used by enrichment so the mirror reproduces the engine's f32 repr. Either way, extend the agreement test grid to inclu...

### [medium] A breakpoints factor consisting of only an open-ended breakpoint (empty boundary) silently produces NO output column
- **Location:** `src/haute/_rating.py:136-220 (_breakpoints_to_rules), open-ended tail append at 213-218; consumed by _apply_banding:99-100,130-131`
- _breakpoints_to_rules appends the open-ended tail rule only `if open_ended is not None and prev_boundary is not None`. With a single open-ended breakpoint and no bounded breakpoints, `bounded` is empty, `prev_boundary` stays None, the tail is skipped, and the function returns []. _apply_banding then finds the continuous when/then chain is None (line 130) and returns the frame UNCHANGED — the configured output column is never created. This is a silent passthrough on a misconfigured-but-plausible ...
- **Fix:** When `bounded` is empty but an open_ended breakpoint exists, emit a single unconditional rule assigning the open-ended label to all finite rows (e.g. {'op1':'>=','val1':-inf} is not allowed since non-finite is rejected; instead build a chain that maps everything to the label, or fall back to a categorical-style constant assignment). - **Location:** `src/haute/routes/_optimiser_service.py:1585-1652 (_estimate_scenario_frontier_ranges) + 4537-4605 (_validate_and_project_auto_range) feeding 1366-1406...`

The haute auto-range estimator is unit-blind: `_estimate_scenario_frontier_ranges` sums each quote's achievab...

### [medium] Empty-input batch scoring hardcodes prediction column to Float64, diverging from the Int64 dtype the same model emits on non-empty input (classification hard labels / integer-valued regression)
- **Location:** `src/haute/_model_scorer.py:1393-1403 (_batch_score_to_parquet zero-row branch)`
- In the zero-row branch of _batch_score_to_parquet, the output prediction column is created as pl.Series(output_col, [], dtype=pl.Float64) unconditionally (line 1401), and the proba column likewise as Float64 (line 1403). So the SAME ScoringModel produ...

Derive the correct output dtype the same way the non-empty path does: either (a) run the model's predict on a synthetic 1-row frame built from the input schema and take prediction.dtype (then write a 0-row column of that dtype), or (b) carry the realised dtype from the first non-emp...

### [medium] CatBoost classification SHAP explanation reports headline prediction_value as the scored HARD LABEL (0/1) while contributions/base reconstruct the LOG-ODDS, producing a meaningless output_difference and a waterfall that cannot reconcile to the displayed prediction
- **Location:** `src/haute/_model_explainability.py:206-214,261-279 (explain_catboost_prediction classification branch)`

The caller explain_model_score_from_config passes prediction_value = the scored 'prediction' column value, which for a CatBoost classifier is the HARD LABEL (0 or 1) per the known scored-output semantics. Because output_value=_as_float(prediction_value, strict=False) is non-None (=1.0), the funct...

Options: (1) For classification, do NOT treat the scored hard label as a value comparable to log-odds: either set prediction_value to the positive-class probability (sigmoid of the raw-formula sum, matching what the *_proba column carries) and report prediction_space='probability', OR keep raw-f...

### [medium] Negative base raised to a fractional power yields a Python complex number where Polars yields NaN
- **Location:** `src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1616-1635 (_binop, ast.Pow -> operator.pow)`
- _binop evaluates ** via operator.pow. For a negative base with a non-integer exponent (e.g. (-8.0) ** 0.5) Python returns a complex number, whereas Polars (float pow) returns NaN. The evaluator therefore stores a complex value as result_value and propagates it into substituted_text / the user-facing calculation. Besides being a value Polars never produces, a complex result can break downstream numeric formatting/serialisation of the trace payload (JSON does not encode complex), or display nonsen...
- **Fix:** In _binop's Pow branch, mirror Polars float semantics: if the result is complex (isinstance(result, complex)) or the base is negative with a non-integral exponent, return float('nan') instead of the complex value. Equivalently compute via math.pow and convert its ValueError (negative base, fractional exp) into nan. Add a test for (-8.0)**0.5 == nan...

### [medium] clip() applies lower-then-upper (min(max(val,lower),upper)); diverges from Polars whenever lower_bound > upper_bound
- **Location:** `src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1781-1802 (_call .clip branch: max(val,lower) then min(val,upper))`
- The evaluator implements clip as: if lower is not None: val = max(val, lower); if upper is not None: val = min(val, upper) — i.e. min(max(val,lower),upper), so the UPPER bound always wins on a contradictory pair. Polars' clip applies the bounds in the opposite effective order (the lower bound dominates for the lower>upper case), so for lower_bound=10, upper_bound=0 the evaluator and Polars disagree. While lower>upper is itself a misconfiguration, clip bounds in rating are frequently column-deriv...
- **Fix:** Match Polars' bound-precedence for the contradictory case. Simplest robust form consistent with Polars observed behaviour: apply upper first then lower — val = min(val, upper) if upper is not None else val; val = max(val, lower) if lower is not None else val. Add a regression test with lower=10, upper=0 across val in {-3,5,50} asserting equality wi...

### [medium] log()/sqrt() (and domain-guarded ops) return None where Polars returns NaN or -inf
- **Location:** `src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1887-1899 (_call .log returns None for val<=0; .sqrt returns None for val<0)`
- For out-of-domain inputs the evaluator returns None: .log() guards `if val>0` (else None), .sqrt() guards `if val>=0` (else None). Polars instead returns float NaN for sqrt(negative) and log(negative), and -inf for log(0). None and NaN/-inf are different values with different downstream meaning (null vs not-a-number vs negative infinity); a trace that shows None for sqrt(-4) misrepresents the real pipeline value (NaN), which in turn flows into is_nan()/fill_nan() logic differently than null flow...
- **Fix:** Return math.nan for log(val<0)/sqrt(val<0) and -math.inf for log(0.0) instead of None, matching Polars float semantics; keep None only when the input itself is null. (Note this distinct from log(0)->-inf which must not be None either.) Add tests for sqrt(-4)==nan, log(0)==-inf, log(-1)==nan, and that a true null input still yields None.

### [medium] max_horizontal/min_horizontal use Python max()/min(), whose NaN handling is argument-order-dependent and diverges from Polars
- **Location:** `src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1990-2017 (_eval_horizontal: max(non_none)/min(non_none) via Python builtins)`
- _eval_horizontal computes max_horizontal/min_horizontal as Python builtin max(non_none)/min(non_none). Python's max/min with a NaN element are order-dependent and return whichever way the comparison short-circuits: max([nan,5])=nan but max([5,nan])=5; min([nan,5])=nan but min([5,nan])=5. So the evaluator's horizontal max/min result depends on the textual order of the arguments and is non-deterministic relative to Polars. Polars has a fixed rule (max_horizontal(nan,5)=nan, min_horizontal(nan,5)=5...
- **Fix:** Stop using bare Python max/min for float horizontal reductions. Reproduce Polars NaN semantics explicitly: if any non-null value is NaN, return NaN for max_horizontal (Polars treats NaN as the max) and ignore NaN for min_horizontal (or whatever the audited Polars rule is per dtype), making the result independent of argument order. At minimum, detec...

### [medium] Division/floor-div/mod by zero raises in Python and is masked to the observed value, but the correct Polars value is +inf (float div) or null (int floordiv) — concrete new instance of the catch-all mask
- **Location:** `src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1616-1635 (_binop Div/FloorDiv/Mod via operator) + 1528-1539 (_compute_result except->row_values.get(target))`
- Polars float division by zero yields +inf/-inf (1.0/0.0 -> inf, including for integer columns since / promotes to Float64), and integer floor-division/modulo by zero yields null. The evaluator computes these via operator.truediv/floordiv/mod, which raise ZeroDivisionError; the exception is swallowed by _compute_result's `except Exception: return row_values.get(target_column)` (line 1538-1539), so the trace displays the ALREADY-OBSERVED row value as if computed. The catch-all masking is catalogue...
- **Fix:** Handle zero divisors explicitly in _binop to mirror Polars: truediv by 0.0 -> math.copysign(inf, numerator) (and nan for 0/0); floordiv/mod by integer 0 -> None (Polars null). This also removes one reliance on the value-laundering catch-all. Add tests: 1.0/0.0==inf, -1.0/0.0==-inf, 0.0/0.0 is nan, 1//0 is None.

Add an optional, declared per-output rounding spec (e.g. on the Output node / deploy manifest: column -> decimal places + ROUND_HALF_EVEN or insurance-standard ROUND_HALF_UP) and apply it in _quote_response...

### [medium] Same-row-count fast path accepts a positionally-aligned parent row whenever the CARRIED shared columns match, with no uniqueness guard — a reorder over rows that are duplicates on the carried key shows the wrong upstream input value
- **Location:** `src/haute/_trace_correlation.py:632-647 (_correlate_rows_posthoc fast path) with _trace_values_match:93-121`
- When len(parent_df)==child_len, the fast path takes parent_df.row(child_row_idx), keeps the columns shared with the child (match_row), and accepts the row if all(_trace_values_match(candidate[c], match_row[c])) over those shared columns. The shared columns are exactly the ones that survived downstream; the parent's OTHER columns (the upstream inputs the trace will DISPLAY) are not checked. If a node reorders rows (sort/group-stable reshuffle) AND two parent rows are identical on the carried/shar...
- **Fix:** Before accepting the positional candidate, require that the carried shared columns uniquely identify the row among parent rows (or that no OTHER parent row also matches match_row on the shared columns); when the carried key is non-unique, fall through to the value-matching path (which already records ambiguity diagnostics) instead of trusting posit...

### [low] Multiple open-ended breakpoints silently drop all but the last; the duplicate-boundary guard only covers bounded boundaries
- **Location:** `src/haute/_rating.py:153-178 (_breakpoints_to_rules boundary/open-ended separation and duplicate guard)`
- The loop assigns `open_ended = bp` on every empty-boundary breakpoint, so if a config contains two or more open-ended breakpoints only the LAST survives and the earlier ones are discarded with no error. The explicit duplicate-boundary rejection (lines 173-178) only checks bounded numeric boundaries via `seen_boundaries`, so it does not catch duplicate/contradictory open-ended tails. A user who accidentally leaves two rows with blank boundaries gets a silently different banding than configured.
- **Fix:** Reject more than one open-ended breakpoint with a ValueError ('a breakpoints factor may have at most one open-ended boundary'), consistent with the existing duplicate-bounded-boundary rejection. Add a regression test feeding two blank-boundary rows and asserting the error.

### [low] Decimal factor columns canonicalise with their declared scale, so a money/Decimal factor only matches string entries written to that exact scale (e.g. '25.50', not '25.5' or '25')

### [medium] detectGaps tests never exercise overlapping/nested rules, so the predecessor-upper (not running-max) bug (V073) is uncovered
- **Location:** `frontend/src/panels/editors/banding/__tests__/bandingUtils.test.ts:151-210`
- Verified bug V073: `detectGaps` compares each sorted interval's upper bound against only its immediate predecessor's upper (`intervals[i].interval.upper`), not the running maximum upper seen so far. When rules overlap or nest (e.g. a wide rule [0,100] followed by a narrow nested rule [10,20] and then a far rule (>50)), the algorithm reports spurious or wrong gaps because a contained interval's small upper bound resets the comparison baseline. Every test in the detectGaps block uses cleanly order...
- **Fix:** Add a test with a nested/overlapping set, e.g. rules [>=0,<=100], [>=10,<=20], [>50] and assert detectGaps returns [] (fully covered, no spurious gap). Add a second case where a genuine gap exists beyond an overlapping pair and assert only the real gap is reported. After fixing detectGaps to track running-max upper, these pin V073.

### [low] FingerprintCache concurrency test only checks the result is a dict, so it cannot detect torn/inconsistent slot state
- **Location:** `tests/test_fingerprint_cache.py:183-210 (TestThreadSafety::test_concurrent_store_and_get)`
- The concurrency test runs two writers (both storing the SAME fingerprint 'fp1' with `{"a": i}` payloads) and two readers, but the reader's only check is `if result is not None and not isinstance(result, dict): errors.append(...)`. Because all writers use the same key and a dict value, the only failure this can catch is a non-dict return or an exception. It never asserts internal consistency (e.g. that a returned multi-slot entry is coherent, that the inner value is one of the stored payloads, or...
- **Fix:** Strengthen the readers to assert the returned entry is internally coherent: store DISTINCT keys per writer with a checksum relationship between slots (e.g. slot_b == f(slot_a)) and assert the relationship holds on every read, and assert len(cache) never exceeds max_entries. This turns the test into a real torn-state detector.

### [low] Non-convergence warning test is a conditional no-op when the solve converges
- **Location:** `tests/test_optimiser_routes.py:4874-4887 (TestNonConvergenceWarning.test_solve_non_convergence_warning)`
- The test runs a real solve with max_iter=1 intending to force non-convergence, then asserts the warning only inside `if not result['converged']:`. If the solve happens to converge in one iteration (trivial inputs, or any future solver/data drift), the guard is False and the test asserts nothing — it passes vacuously without ever verifying the non-convergence warning it exists to check. This is the classic conditional-assertion anti-pattern: the test cannot fail to catch a regression where the wa...
- **Fix:** Make non-convergence deterministic and assert it unconditionally: construct inputs/config that provably cannot converge in the iteration budget, assert result['converged'] is False, then assert 'warning' in result (and ideally assert the warning text). Remove the `if` guard so a missing warning on real non-convergence fails the test.

### [low] useMlflowBrowser tests never simulate out-of-order async resolution, so the stale-response race (V082) is uncovered

### [medium] JSON apiInput cache build fully materialises the file as Python objects with ~3-4x peak-memory copies, defeating the streaming iterator; bounded only by a wall-clock timeout
- **Location:** `src/haute/_json_shred.py:825-826 (build_per_port_cache) and :672-729 (_buffer_to_frame)`

### [medium] upstream_node_ids uses a Python list as a FIFO with stack.pop(0) and stack[0:0]=... — both O(n) shifts, making the traversal O(V*E) on wide/deep lineages
- **Location:** `src/haute/_graph_utils.py:31-46 (upstream_node_ids)`
- The BFS over parents uses stack.pop(0) (line 41) and stack[0:0] = parents_of.get(current, []) (line 45). list.pop(0) is O(len(stack)) because it shifts every remaining element left, and slice-assignment at index 0 (stack[0:0]=...) is O(len(stack)+len(inserted)) for the same reason. The frontier can hold O(V) ids for a wide graph, so the traversal degrades from the expected O(V+E) to O(V*E) in the worst case. This function is invoked inside _upstream_subgraph for every cache-key build (see findin...
- **Fix:** Use collections.deque (popleft() is O(1)) and extend()/extendleft() instead of list.pop(0) and slice assignment. The result list is order-significant ('nearest parents first') but a deque preserves the same visitation order with O(1) ends. This is a drop-in change with no behavioural difference.

Validate lazily/cheaply: gate the expensive collect_schema() check behind a fast stat (size+mtime) comparison against the stored metadata, and skip re-validation entirely for an entry validated within the same operation. At minimum, drop self._lock before the collect_schema() I/O (hold it only to read...

## Type & invariant design (63)

### [high] PipelineGraph permits a nodes list with duplicate ids; node_map silently collapses them (uniqueness is not a type invariant)
- **Location:** `src/haute/_types.py:638-705 (PipelineGraph.nodes + node_map cached_property)`
- PipelineGraph.nodes is a plain `list[GraphNode]` with no model_validator enforcing id-uniqueness. The canonical `node_map` is built as `{n.id: n for n in self.nodes}` (line 700), which silently drops all but the last node sharing an id, and `parents_of`/topo/cache-key code all assume `node_map` is a total bijection over `nodes`. So a `PipelineGraph` where two GraphNodes share an id is a representable-but-illegal state: every id-keyed lookup (executor node_map, projection, fingerprint, deploy) op...
- **Fix:** Add a Pydantic `@model_validator(mode="after")` on PipelineGraph that raises if `len({n.id for n in self.nodes}) != len(self.nodes)`, naming the duplicate ids. This makes the collapse unconstructable and lets node_map/parents_of/fingerprint code rely on `nodes` and `node_map` having identical cardinality. (Independently, the parser should be fixed ...

### [high] active_source not constrained to be a member of sources on PipelineGraph, though the identical invariant IS enforced on SidecarModel
- **Location:** `src/haute/_types.py:656-657 (sources/active_source) vs src/haute/routes/_helpers.py:60-70 (SidecarModel validator)`

### [low] position modelled as open dict[str, float] permitting missing/extra keys; readers defensively `.get('x')` and tolerate None
- **Location:** `src/haute/_types.py:602-607 (GraphNode.position) and access at src/haute/routes/_helpers.py:788-789 (position.get('x'))`
- `position: dict[str, float]` with a default factory of `{"x": 0.0, "y": 0.0}` does not require the x/y keys nor forbid extras. routes/_helpers.py:788-789 reads it as `position.get("x")`/`position.get("y")` — i.e. the consumer assumes the keys may be absent and accepts `None`. So `GraphNode(id=..., position={})` or `{"x": 1.0}` or `{"x":1,"y":2,"z":3}` are all representable, and a half-populated position flows to downstream code as `(None, y)`. The invariant 'a position is exactly an (x, y) pair ...
- **Fix:** Replace the open dict with a closed model: `class Position(BaseModel): x: float = 0.0; y: float = 0.0` (extras forbidden via `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")`), and type `position: Position`. This makes both fields always-present floats, eliminates the `.get(...)/None` defensive reads, and rejects stray keys at the boundary.

### [medium - a 0..1 fraction with no bound permits NaN/inf/negative/>1 values to cross the wire and reach progress bars; a constrained float makes the invariant unrepresentable-if-violated and is consistent with the file's own bounded-int fields] progress is an unconstrained `float` despite being a 0.0..1.0 fraction
- **Location:** `src/haute/schemas.py:480-487 (ExploreStatusResponse.progress:482), 862-876 (TrainStatusResponse.progress:864), 1066-1077 (OptimiserFrontierAutoRangeSt...`
- Four status models declare `progress: float = 0.0`. Producers feed it fractional values (e.g. _explore_service.py:773 `progress=0.1`, :792 `progress=0.85`) and the UI renders it as a 0..100% bar. There is no `ge=0, le=1` constraint, so a buggy producer (or a job-store dict that was never coerced — values come via `job.get('progress', 0.0)` at modelling.py:111, optimiser.py:1230, etc.) can emit a negative, >1, NaN, or inf progress and Pydantic will happily serialise it, overflowing or NaN-poisoni...
- **Fix:** Define a shared `Progress = Annotated[float, Field(ge=0.0, le=1.0, allow_inf_nan=False)]` and use it for all four progress fields (and `OptimiserHistoryEntry`/other 0..1 ratio fields where applicable). This makes an out-of-range progress unrepresentable on the wire and forces producers to clamp at the source.

## Security (10)

### [critical] RestrictedUnpickler allowlist admits real RCE gadgets via whole-package numpy entry — arbitrary code execution through safe_unpickle AND safe_joblib_load (not merely object construction)
- **Location:** `src/haute/_sandbox.py:346-394 (_ALLOWED_PICKLE_PREFIXES single-segment ('numpy',) entry + _pickle_global_is_allowed prefix match); gate at 405-413 (_R...`
- The pickle allowlist is built from whole-package single-segment prefixes. _pickle_global_is_allowed('numpy', <anything>) and _pickle_global_is_allowed('numpy.<any.submodule>', <anything>) return True — find_class filters ONLY by module prefix, never by qualname. The numpy package tree contains direct code-execution primitives reachable as pickle globals, and pickle's REDUCE opcode invokes whatever callable find_class returns with attacker-controlled arguments. The cleanest gadget is numpy.testin...
- **Fix:** Stop allowlisting by module prefix; whole-package entries are unsafe because large library trees inevitably contain exec/subprocess/loader gadgets (numpy.testing.runstring, numpy.distutils.exec_command, numpy.ctypeslib.load_library, pandas.core.computation.eval.eval, etc.). Replace _ALLOWED_PICKLE_PREFIXES with an explicit, exhaustive set of (modul...

### [high] Route input-path guard omits modelScore feature_contract_path / artifact_path, so even guarded preview/trace/sink routes read an out-of-root file with zero containment
- **Location:** `src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:118-156 (_RUNTIME_INPUT_PATH_CONFIG_BY_NODE_TYPE / _validate_runtime_input_paths); src/haute/execution.py:118-126 & 512-5...`

Its coverage map `_RUNTIME_INPUT_PATH_CONFIG_BY_NODE_TYPE` does NOT include MODEL_SCORE at all. Yet the executor's own field map `_LOCAL_RUNTIME_INPUT_PATH_FIELDS_BY_NODE_TYPE[NodeType.MODEL_SCORE] = ('artifact_path','feature_contract_pat...
- **Fix:** Make containment a property of the execution boundary, not a per-route allow-map. Concretely: (1) replace the route guard's hand-maintained `_RUNTIME_INPUT_PATH_CONFIG_BY_NODE_TYPE` with the executor's authoritative `_runtime_file_signature_paths` / `_runtime_input_path_fields` enumeration so the guard validates exactly the fields the engine reads ...

Its value is fully attacker-controlled in the shared/imported-pipeline trust model: it is either submodels[name]["file"] (an arbitrary string on the PipelineGraph) or, whe...

This makes the value a proper Python string literal that cannot break out, e...

### [medium] AST blocklist bypassed by str.format / format_map / format() template attribute traversal — arbitrary attribute read (closure secrets, __globals__) from shared/node pipeline code
- **Location:** `src/haute/_sandbox.py:239-243 (visit_Call only inspects ast.Name callees), 229-237 (visit_Attribute only sees literal dunder tokens), 102-109 ('format...`
- The AST validator's entire dunder defence rests on seeing dunder names as literal `ast.Attribute.attr` tokens (`_BLOCKED_ATTRS`, `_BLOCKED_FRAME_ATTRS`) and blocked builtins as literal `ast.Name` call targets (`_BLOCKED_CALLS`). Python's string-formatting mini-language performs full attribute AND index traversal driven by a runtime *template string* that the AST never parses. `"{0.__closure__[0].cell_contents}".format(obj)`, `"{x.__globals__}".format_map({'x': obj})`, and `format(obj, spec)` the...
- **Fix:** Treat string-formatting attribute traversal as a first-class escape vector. Minimal hardening: (1) In `_ASTValidator.visit_Call`, also reject calls whose callee is an `ast.Attribute` with `attr in {'format', 'format_map', 'format_field', 'vformat', 'get_field'}` (the str/Formatter traversal surface), and add `format` to `_BLOCKED_BUILTINS`/`_BLOCKE...

### [medium] SPA index route serves the live session token to any client without Origin or token authentication, escalating the documented 'leakable secret' into turnkey unauthenticated token disclosure once bound non-loopback
- **Location:** `src/haute/server.py:707-730 (serve_spa + _serve_index_html); gate-bypass roots at src/haute/_local_security.py:173 (LocalSessionMiddleware only guards...`
- The whole local-auth design rests on a single per-process secret: every /api/* route and the /ws/sync WebSocket require the session token (LocalSessionMiddleware._token_matches / websocket_rejection_reason). But the token is itself dispensed, with NO authentication, by the SPA catch-all. _serve_index_html() (server.py:707-719) embeds the live token verbatim as `<script>window.__HAUTE_SESSION_TOKEN__ = "<token>"</script>`, and serve_spa (server.py:721-730) returns that HTML for `/` and EVERY non-...
- **Fix:** Do not dispense the session token from an unauthenticated endpoint. Preferred: stop embedding the token in HTML entirely and instead set it as an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Secure-where-applicable cookie scoped to the loopback host, issued only on a request that already proves locality, and have the frontend send it back automatically (cookie) or f...

### [medium] Databricks SELECT-clause validator is a keyword denylist that does NOT constrain which tables a query reads — a shared pipeline's `query` field can exfiltrate any table the operator's token can reach (the table-name allowlist is a false guarantee)
- **Location:** `src/haute/_databricks_io.py:63-84 (_validate_select_clause) and :303-308 (sql_query = f"{select_clause} FROM {table}"); reachable via src/haute/routes...`

That validator is a pure denylist: it requires a leading SELECT, forbids `;`, `--`, `/*`, and the keywords DROP/DELETE/INSERT/UPDATE/ALTER/TRUNCATE/EXEC/EXECUTE/CREATE/GRANT/REVOKE/UNION/LATERAL. It performs NO restriction on which tables the SELECT b...
- **Fix:** Stop relying on a keyword denylist over free-form SQL. Preferred: drop the arbitrary `query` field for the fetch path and build the statement from structured, validated parts only — e.g. an explicit allowlisted column list plus an optional WHERE built from parameterised predicates, with every table reference forced to equal the allowlisted `table` ...

### [low] AST validator is a denylist with several reachable-but-unblocked dunders (__getattribute__, __base__, __subclasshook__, __class_getitem__, __getstate__/__setstate__, __init__) — fragile by construction
- **Location:** `src/haute/_sandbox.py:135-160 (_BLOCKED_ATTRS denylist) and 214-270 (_ASTValidator denylist design)`

I empirically confirmed (against the real validator) that the following dunders PASS validation today: `__getattribute__` (the sibling of the blocked `__getattr__`, and a general attribute-read primitive), `__base__` (singular form of the blocked `__bases__`), `__subclasshook__`, `__class_getitem__`, `__getstat...
- **Fix:** Invert the model to an ALLOWLIST where feasible: in `visit_Attribute`, reject ALL names matching `^__.*__$` except a small vetted allowlist of provably-safe dunders actually needed by pipeline code (e.g. `__len__`); this single change subsumes the entire current `_BLOCKED_ATTRS` set and closes the whole future-dunder class at once (the existing tes...

### [low] _normalise_path_text backslash->slash rewrite diverges from the executor read guard on POSIX and corrupts Windows extended-length/device paths
- **Location:** `src/haute/_path_resolution.py:11-21 (_normalise_path_text) used by resolve_runtime_file_path:86-99; contrast src/haute/_io.py:141-147 (_validate_sourc...`
- `_normalise_path_text` unconditionally does `text.replace('\\','/')` on every platform before constructing the Path. This creates two problems. (A) Cross-platform divergence: on POSIX a backslash is a legal filename character, so the route guard `resolve_runtime_file_path` (which calls `_normalise_path_text`) interprets `..\..\etc\passwd` as the traversal `../../etc/passwd` (`..` IS in parts), whereas the executor's actual flat-file read guard `_io._validate_source_path` does `Path(path_string)....
- **Fix:** Do not rewrite backslashes on POSIX. Gate the replacement on `os.name == 'nt'` (or use `os.sep`/`PureWindowsPath` only when running on Windows), and reject UNC/extended-length/device prefixes explicitly rather than mangling them. Make the route guard and `_io._validate_source_path` share a single normalisation+containment helper so the two boundari...

### [low] is_relative_to containment is case-sensitive on POSIX but macOS/APFS is case-insensitive, so case-variant paths in a shared pipeline can bypass containment on macOS
- **Location:** `src/haute/_sandbox.py:60-72 (validate_project_path); src/haute/_path_resolution.py:64-67 & 89-93 (_candidate_if_allowed / absolute branch); src/haute/...`
- All three containment primitives gate on `resolved.is_relative_to(root)`. `pathlib.PurePosixPath.is_relative_to` is case-SENSITIVE (verified: `PurePosixPath('/PROJ/secret').is_relative_to('/proj')` is False), while `PureWindowsPath.is_relative_to` is case-insensitive. On Windows, `Path.resolve()` also canonicalises case to the real on-disk casing (verified), so case games do not help an attacker there. On macOS, however, the default APFS volume is case-INSENSITIVE while Python treats paths with ...

## API/DX & error messages (39)

### [high] run()/score() silently return the LAST topological node, not a declared output — fan-outs return the wrong DataFrame
- **Location:** `src/haute/pipeline.py:360 (run, `return outputs[order[-1].name]`) and src/haute/pipeline.py:409 (score, same)`
- Both run() and score() end with `return outputs[order[-1].name]`, i.e. they return whichever node sorts last in topological order. For any graph that is not a strict linear chain this is a silent footgun. Empirically confirmed: a pipeline with `src -> out` (the real result, an @output node) AND `src -> sink` (a @data_sink that writes a side file) returns the SINK's frame, because `sink` happens to sort after `out`. The user gets a wrong-but-plausible DataFrame with no error, no warning, and no w...
- **Fix:** Make the returned frame explicit. Resolve output as: (1) if exactly one node has NodeType.OUTPUT (or is a sink leaf with no consumers), return it; (2) if multiple terminal/output nodes exist, raise a HauteError naming them and instructing the user to mark one (e.g. via an `output=True` kwarg or by being the sole @output node); (3) only fall back to...

### [high] Node.__call__ silently drops extra wired inputs (single-param node fed two edges uses only the first)
- **Location:** `src/haute/pipeline.py:54-68 (Node.__call__), specifically the `dfs[0]` single-input path (line 67) and the `dfs[: self.n_inputs]` slice (line 64)`
- Node.__call__ decides how many inputs to pass purely from the function signature's parameter count, then SLICES the incoming dfs to that count: a single-param node uses `dfs[0]` (line 67) and a multi-param node uses `dfs[: self.n_inputs]` (line 64). If the user wires MORE edges into a node than its function accepts, the surplus inputs are silently discarded. Empirically confirmed: `@p.polars def t(df): return df` with `p.connect('a','t').connect('b','t')` runs successfully and silently uses only...
- **Fix:** Validate edge count against the node's declared arity at run/score time (or at connect/finalisation). When `len(dfs) > n_inputs` for a non-source node, raise a HauteError naming the node, the number of connected inputs, the number the function accepts, and the offending extra source(s). Only slice when counts match. This makes over-wiring fail loud...

- handle_train checks only `filepath.exists()` (line 64) — which is True for a directory — then calls `validate_user_code(read_user_text(filepath), allow_imports=True)` at line 74. `read_user_text` (src/haute/_io.py:461) does `Path(path).read_text(...)`, which raises `IsADirectoryError` for a directory and `PermissionError` for an unreadable path. ### [low] `haute run` writes per-node FAILURE lines to stdout but the failure summary to stderr
- **Location:** `src/haute/cli/_run.py:67 vs src/haute/cli/_run.py:70`
- In handle_run, the per-node result loop prints both success and failure lines via `click.echo(...)` to STDOUT (line 64 for the ✓ line, line 67 `click.echo(f"  ✗ {nid}: {res.error ...}")` for the ✗ failure line). Only the trailing summary `click.echo(f"\n{errors} node(s) failed.", err=True)` (line 70) goes to stderr. So the actual diagnostic — which node failed and why — lands on stdout while the count lands on stderr. A caller that captures stderr to surface errors (a common CI/script pattern) s...
- **Fix:** Route the per-node failure line (line 67) to stderr (`err=True`) so the failing-node diagnostics and the failure summary share the same stream, while keeping the ✓ success lines and the final data preview on stdout. This matches the stdout=data / stderr=diagnostics convention the other commands follow.

- PreambleError(HauteError) overrides __init__ to accept and store source_line, but it stores it as a plain attribute (self.source_line = source_line) and calls super().__init__(message) WITHOUT passing source_line into HauteError's **context mechanism. ## Dependency / supply-chain (37)

### [high] pyjwt
- **Location:** `pyjwt`
- No direct action; it rides along when databricks-sql-connector updates. Current `databricks-sql-connector>=4.2.5,<5` already permits a pyjwt>=2.13 resolution on `uv lock --upgrade`. Treat as transitive defense-in-depth, not a haute exposure.

### [high] gitpython
- **Location:** `gitpython`
- Closed transitively by the mlflow 3.11.x bump (P4). No standalone action; not a haute-reachable RCE.

### [high] pillow
- **Location:** `pillow`
- None required for security. Optionally allow it to update on `uv lock --upgrade` (matplotlib has no upper bound here). All 6 are non-issues for haute.

### [high] starlette
- **Location:** `starlette`
- BLOCKED by pin: `fastapi>=0.129,<0.130` caps starlette at 0.52.x, so the starlette>=1.0 fixes cannot be pulled without a fastapi major upgrade (outside the current envelope). Accept-with-mitigation now (loopback default + TrustedHost + path-only URL use); track a coordinated fastapi+starlette bump as a follow-up. Do NOT loosen the fastapi bound ad hoc.

### [high] python-multipart
- **Location:** `python-multipart`
- P1: bump `python-multipart>=0.0.31,<0.1` (well within the existing `<0.1` upper bound). Highest-value DIRECT-dependency fix in this audit; closes all 5 CVEs at once.

### [medium] python-dotenv
- **Location:** `python-dotenv`
- P1 (cheap): bump `python-dotenv>=1.2.2,<2` (within `<2` bound). Trivial and removes the symlink-overwrite primitive even though haute uses the read path.

### [medium] cryptography
- **Location:** `cryptography`
- P2: include in `uv lock --upgrade` to reach >=46.0.7 (or 48.x for the OpenSSL fix). Only relevant when the databricks extra is installed; defense-in-depth for the TLS stack.

### [medium] idna
- **Location:** `idna`
- P2: `uv lock --upgrade` to idna>=3.15 (no upper bound constrains it). Low urgency.

### [medium] pyasn1
- **Location:** `pyasn1`
- P2: bump on `uv lock --upgrade` to >=0.6.3. Defense-in-depth; only present with the databricks extra.

### [medium] urllib3
- **Location:** `urllib3`
- P2: `uv lock --upgrade` to urllib3>=2.7.0. Defense-in-depth for the HTTP stack.

### [medium] werkzeug
- **Location:** `werkzeug`
- Closed by mlflow 3.11.x bump (P4). No haute action; non-issue.

### [medium] mako
- **Location:** `mako`
- Closed by mlflow/alembic update under the 3.11.x bump (P4). No haute action; non-issue.

### [medium] pymdown-extensions
- **Location:** `pymdown-extensions`
- P3: update with the docs toolchain. Non-issue for production.

### [low] requests
- **Location:** `requests`
- P2/none: let it update on `uv lock --upgrade`. Non-issue for haute (helper not called).

### [low] flask
- **Location:** `flask`
- Closed by mlflow 3.11.x bump (P4). No haute action; non-issue.

### [low] pygments
- **Location:** `pygments`
- P3: bump on next dev-tooling refresh (no pin pins pygments directly; it follows mkdocs-material/pytest). Non-issue for production.

### [low] pytest
- **Location:** `pytest`
- P3: bump the pin to `pytest==9.0.3`. Trivial; non-issue for production.

### [low] @babel/core
- **Location:** `@babel/core`
- `npm update @babel/core` to the patched 7.x. Optionally align the directly-pinned @babel/parser/traverse/types to the same patched minor for consistency.

### [critical (server CVEs) / medium (client tmp+hash)] mlflow
- **Location:** `mlflow`
- Defer to a databricks-integration pass: bump to mlflow>=3.11.0,<4 (within current `mlflow<4` bound) when next touching the extra; this also pulls fixed flask/werkzeug/gitpython/mako transitively. Not urgent because haute is an mlflow client, not server.

### [n/a] (item)
- `fastapi>=0.129.0,<0.130` transitively caps starlette at 0.52.x. The starlette CVE fixes that matter (host-spoof, StaticFiles, urlencoded limit bypass) land in starlette>=1.0.1/1.1.0/1.3.1, which fastapi 0.129 does not allow. So the single most security-relevant upper bound in pyproject is currently blocking the starlette patches.

### [n/a] (item)
- Two DIRECT core deps ship vulnerable floors that have free in-range fixes: `python-multipart>=0.0.22,<0.1` (fix 0.0.31 is < 0.1) and `python-dotenv>=1.2.1,<2` (fix 1.2.2 is < 2). The upper bounds are fine; only the resolved/lower versions are stale.

### [n/a] (item)
- cryptography (46.0.7), idna (3.15), pyasn1 (0.6.3), requests (2.33.0), urllib3 (2.7.0) all have fixes with NO blocking upper bound in pyproject (they are unpinned transitives) but the lock is pinned to older versions.

### [n/a] (item)
- The entire mlflow/databricks stack (mlflow x8, pyjwt x11, gitpython x4, flask, werkzeug, mako, cryptography x5, pyasn1, plus heavy transitives: alembic, docker, graphene/graphql, sqlalchemy via alembic, gunicorn-free flask server) is pulled ONLY by the optional `databricks` extra. It is, however, force-installed in dependency-groups.dev via `haute[databricks]`, so every developer/CI env resolves the full vulnerable set even though production installs without the extra are clean.

### [n/a] (item)
- pillow (12MB+, 6 CVEs) and the matplotlib stack reach the CORE install via catboost->matplotlib, but haute imports NO matplotlib/PIL and never plots or decodes images. matplotlib is also pulled by mlflow. It is effectively dead weight on the core runtime that nonetheless contributes 6 CVEs to the audit headline.

### [n/a] (item)
- AGPL-3.0-only project. Reviewed the resolved dependency set: fastapi/starlette/pydantic/uvicorn/click/orjson/pillow (MIT/HPND), polars (MIT), catboost/mlflow/databricks-sdk/databricks-sql-connector/cryptography/protobuf (Apache-2.0), gitpython/joblib/numpy/prettytable/lz4/pyasn1 (BSD), pyjwt (MIT). No GPL-2.0-only (incompatible-with-AGPLv3-as-licensee-imposed), no commercial/proprietary, and no SSPL/BUSL packages observed in the lock.

## Frontend a11y/bundle/UX (24)

### [high] Preview-table cells (the trace entry point) are keyboard- and screen-reader-inaccessible
- **Location:** `frontend/src/panels/DataPreview.tsx:139-168 (DataCell <td>), :221-234 (handleCellClick), :340 (<tbody onClick>), :360-368 (DataCell render)`
- The core 'click a cell to trace how this value was computed' interaction is implemented as an onClick delegated on <tbody> (line 340) that resolves the target <td data-row-index data-column> (lines 221-234). The cell itself is a plain <td> (DataCell, lines 147-167) with cursor:pointer when clickable but NO tabIndex, NO role="button", and NO onKeyDown. There is no other affordance anywhere to start a trace. Consequently a keyboard-only or screen-reader user cannot reach or activate any cell: the ...
- **Fix:** Make traceable cells real interactive elements: when onCellClick is provided, render each DataCell with role="button", tabIndex={0}, an aria-label like `Trace ${column} = ${formattedValue}, row ${rowIndex+1}`, and an onKeyDown that fires the same trace on Enter/Space (preventDefault on Space). Keep the existing delegated onClick for mouse users, or...

### [high] Node-editor form controls have no programmatic label (visual-only labels, no htmlFor/aria-label)
- **Location:** `frontend/src/panels/editors/* and frontend/src/panels/modelling/* (e.g. GLMTargetConfig.tsx:64-101 selects; ConstantEditor.tsx:31-64 inputs; DataSourc...`
- Across the node editors and modelling/optimiser config panels, the pervasive pattern is a visual <label> rendered as a sibling of its <input>/<select>/<textarea> with neither an htmlFor on the label nor an id/aria-label/aria-labelledby on the control. Grep across frontend/src/panels: 146 <label> elements, only 8 use htmlFor (and those 8 are in BandingEditor/RatingStepEditor/GenerateBandsDialog); ~73 form controls in panels/editors alone. Concrete confirmations: GLMTargetConfig.tsx Target/Weight/...

Cheapest consistent route: give EditorLabel a required htmlFor and have each editor pass a useId()-generated id that is also set on the control (mirrors the existing ConfigCheckbox pattern). Where a wrapping <label> is acceptable, wrap the control. For toggle/select groups already announced via role, use aria...

### [high] WebSocket disconnect has no banner and no recovery affordance — live-sync silently dies behind a 2px dot

### [high] Connection-status dot is colour-only and not exposed to assistive tech (no role/aria-live, hover-title only)

### [medium] WebSocket/sync error banner is not announced (sibling session-expired banner is)

The immediately adjacent syncBanner (lines 533-539) — which carries live-sync/WebSocket failure messages — is a plain <div> with no role="alert"/aria-live, so when the editor loses its connection to the server the message appears silently for screen-reader users. Given this banner communicates that the user's edits may no longer be syncing, missing the announc...

Give the dismiss button aria-label="Dismiss" and render the close glyph from a proper icon (lucide X) or an explicit ×, and add aria-hidden to the decorative glyph.

### [medium] Invalid listbox/option ARIA structure: focusable <button> elements used as role=option inside a combobox listbox
- **Location:** `frontend/src/components/NodeSearch.tsx:188-257 (role="listbox" containing role="option" <button> elements)`
- The results container is role="listbox" (line 191) and each result row is a native <button> with role="option" (lines 228-235). The ARIA combobox/listbox pattern in use here keeps DOM focus on the input and drives selection via aria-activedescendant (line 177). Options in that pattern must be non-interactive elements (e.g. <div role="option">); using real <button>s that are themselves in the tab order (buttons are focusable by default) contradicts the activedescendant model and produces an inval...
- **Fix:** Render each result row as a non-focusable <div role="option"> (with the existing id/aria-selected/aria-posinset/aria-setsize and onClick/onMouseEnter handlers) instead of a <button>, matching the off-screen active clone. Keep all keyboard interaction on the input via the existing handleKeyDown + aria-activedescendant. This removes the spurious Tab ...

### [medium] GenerateBandsDialog declares role="dialog" but has no Escape-to-close, no autofocus, and no focus management
- **Location:** `frontend/src/panels/editors/banding/GenerateBandsDialog.tsx:95-105 (role="dialog" with no Escape handler, no focus management)`

(It is an inline elevated card within the banding editor rather than a full overlay, so a hard focus-trap is debatable, but Escape-to-dismiss and initial focus are still expected for anything announced as a dialog.) ### [medium] Panel close buttons rely on title="Close" with a bare icon and no aria-label (inconsistent with the rest of the codebase)
- **Location:** `frontend/src/panels/PanelHeader.tsx:51-58 and frontend/src/panels/NodePanel.tsx:907-911`
- The shared PanelHeader close button (lines 51-58, used by UtilityPanel, ImportsPanel, GitPanel and any PanelShell-titled panel) and the bespoke NodePanel header close button (lines 907-911) render only a decorative lucide <X/> SVG and provide their accessible name solely via the HTML title attribute — no aria-label. The lucide <X> has no aria-hidden, and title is the lowest-priority source in the accessible-name computation and is announced unreliably across screen readers (and not at all to man...
- **Fix:** Add aria-label="Close" (or a more specific label like "Close panel"/"Close node properties") to the PanelHeader close button and the NodePanel header close button, and mark the inner <X> aria-hidden="true". Keeping title for the tooltip is fine, but the accessible name should come from aria-label.

### [medium] Bundle-budget gate's lazy-only modulepreload check cannot detect a heavy panel becoming eager, and the lazy-only allowlist omits the preview/trace panels entirely
- **Location:** `frontend/scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs:20-50 (LAZY_ONLY_MODULEPRELOAD_CHUNK_PREFIXES + isLazyOnlyModulepreloadChunk at 164-168, enforcement at 213-219...`
- The gate has two defenses against eager heavy code: (1) a 240 KiB initial-gzip ceiling, and (2) a LAZY_ONLY_MODULEPRELOAD_CHUNK_PREFIXES check that fails if a known lazy chunk appears as a startup <link rel=modulepreload>. Defense (2) is structurally blind to the most likely regression. ### [medium] Preview error state is visual-only — backend/preview failures are not announced to screen readers
- **Location:** `frontend/src/panels/DataPreview.tsx:280-286 (error block) and 416-421 (header error chip)`
- When a node preview fails (status==='error'), DataPreview renders the error purely as an AlertCircle icon plus styled text inside a plain <div> (lines 280-286), and the header shows a second visual-only error chip (lines 416-421). Neither has role='alert' nor lives in an aria-live region, so a screen-reader user who triggers a preview and gets a backend error (timeout, contract error, Polars exception) receives no spoken feedback — the panel simply appears to do nothing. This is inconsistent wit...
- **Fix:** Add role='alert' to the error container (DataPreview.tsx:280-286) so the failure message is announced when it appears. Consider also wrapping the status header region in an aria-live='polite' so status transitions (ok/error/loading) are conveyed. Ensure the error text is the accessible content (it already is plain text).

### [medium] Loading states ('Loading pipeline…', 'Executing pipeline…') are not announced — no role=status/aria-live

### [low] Toast live-region politeness conflicts with per-toast role=alert, and persistent error toasts lack a clear close path for SR users

- ToastContainer wraps all toasts in a region with aria-live="polite" (line 66) while each ToastItem is role="alert" (line 38), which maps to aria-live="assertive". Ensure error toasts are individually dismissible (already true) and consider making the alert non-persistent in the a11y tree after ...

- App.tsx statically imports OptimiserPreview (824 lines, pulling its whole optimiser/ chart subtree: FrontierChart, ConvergenceChart, SummaryTab, RatebookRatesTab, DetailCard, RatebookImpactBeeswarm), OptimiserDataPreview (768 lines), ExplorePreview (260 lines), and ModellingPreview — all into the initial bundle (App.tsx:23-26). These panels render only conditionally (App.tsx:589-637) when a user opens an optimiser/modelling/explore result, which is a minority of sessions. Meanwhile every NodePan...
- **Fix:** Wrap OptimiserPreview, OptimiserDataPreview, ExplorePreview, and ModellingPreview in React.lazy with a Suspense fallback (reuse the existing LazyEditorBoundary or a small skeleton), matching the NodePanel editor pattern, so their chunks load only when a result of that type is actually displayed.
